\displaystyle\Delta S_{k}=-(1-\epsilon_{k})\ln(1-\epsilon_k)-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}\leq\epsilon_{k}-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k},

(105)

and the dissipation is upper bounded by

\displaystyle W_{\text{diss}}^{\text{sc},*}(N)=W_{\text{diss}}^{\text{sc}}(N)+\beta^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{N}(\epsilon_{k}-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}).

(106)

In the Jaynes-Cummings battery model, the entropy change in round
k
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\Delta S_{k}

\displaystyle=-(1-\epsilon_{k})\ln(1-\epsilon_k)-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}-\epsilon_{k}(\cos^{2}\theta_{k}\ln\cos^{2}\theta_{k}+\sin^{2}\theta_{k}\ln\sin^{2}\theta_{k})

(107)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq 2\epsilon_{k}-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k},

(108)

and the dissipation is upper bounded by

\displaystyle W_{\text{diss}}^{\text{jc},*}(N)=W_{\text{diss}}^{\text{jc}}(N)+\beta^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{N}(2\epsilon_{k}-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}).

(109)

The quantum state tomography algorithm in
[
20
]
ensures a polylogarithmic cumulative infidelity with a high probability. Now we focus on the case where the cumulative infidelity is upper bounded by
\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N
with high probability
1-\delta
, that is, for some constant
C
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}\epsilon_{k}\leq C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N.

(110)

By taking the derivative
(-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k})^{\prime}=-\ln\epsilon_{k}-1
, it is obvious that when
0<\epsilon_{k}\leq e^{-1}
,
-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}
increases with
\epsilon_{k}
while
e^{-1}\leq\epsilon_{k}\leq 1
,
-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}
decreases with
\epsilon_{k}
. Now we consider two cases:

Case 1:
C(\ln(N/\delta)\ln N)/N\leq e^{-1}
.
This happens when
N
is large enough. As a result,
-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}
monotonically increase with respect to
\epsilon_{k}
when
\epsilon_{k}\leq C(\ln(N/\delta)\ln N)/N
. On the one hand, for
\epsilon_{k}\leq C(\ln(N/\delta)\ln N)/N
,

\displaystyle-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}\leq\frac{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}{N}\ln\frac{N}{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N},

(111)

On the other hand, for
\epsilon_{k}>C(\ln(N/\delta)\ln N)/N
,

\displaystyle-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}\leq\epsilon_{k}\ln\frac{N}{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N},

(112)

Then,

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\epsilon_{k}:\epsilon_{k}\leq\frac{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}{N}}\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}-\sum_{\epsilon_{k}:\epsilon_{k}>\frac{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}{N}}\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}

(113)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\epsilon_{k}:\epsilon_{k}\leq\frac{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}{N}}\frac{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}{N}\ln\frac{N}{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}

(114)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\epsilon_{k}:\epsilon_{k}>\frac{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}{N}}\epsilon_{k}\ln\frac{N}{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}

(115)

\displaystyle\leq C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N\ln\frac{N}{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}+C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N\ln\frac{N}{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}

(116)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq 2C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N\ln\frac{N}{C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N}.

(117)

Case 2:
C(\ln(N/\delta)\ln N)/N>e^{-1}
.
This happens when
N
is not very large. Therefore,
N/e\leq C\ln(N/\delta)\ln N
and certainly
N\geq 2
. Using the fact that
-\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}\leq e^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{N}\epsilon_{k}\ln\frac{1}{\epsilon_{k}}\leq\frac{N}{e}\leq C\ln\frac{N}{\delta}\ln N.

(118)

Combining both cases, we conclude that

\displaystyle-\sum_{t=1}^{N}\epsilon_{k}\ln\epsilon_{k}\leq O((\ln N)^{3}).

(119)

Substituting into Eq. (
104
), we obtain the dissipation for for the semi-classical model,

\displaystyle W_{\text{diss}}^{\text{sc},*}(N)\leq W_{\text{diss}}^{\text{sc}}(N)+\beta^{-1}O((\ln N)^{3})=O((\ln N)^{3}).

(120)

and the Jaynes-Cummings battery model,

\displaystyle W_{\text{diss}}^{\text{jc},*}(N)\leq W_{\text{diss}}^{\text{jc}}(N)+2\beta^{-1}O((\ln N)^{3})=O((\ln N)^{3}),

(121)

Therefore, the regret still scales as
O(\polylog(N))
even if we take the energy dissipation due to Landauer’s principle into account.

As mentioned above, in order to achieve Landauer’s bound, the erasure process must necessarily be done quasi-statically. This is not something that a sequential adaptive agent can accomplish since it needs to measure and decide on its action in real time. To circumvent this, an array of empty memory registers can be first prepared. Suppose the agent will be extracting work for
N
steps, we first prepare a memory register
M_{0}
, consisting of
N
empty registers.

M_{0}=\{\underbrace{0,0,\ldots,0}_{N}\}

(122)

At every time step, rather than erasing the old memory that has the previous outcome remembered, the agent simply records the outcome of the measurement on the battery into a new empty register. In order words, after time step
t
, the memory register takes the form

M_{t}=\{r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{t},0,\ldots,0\}\quad\text{for}\quad t\in\{{1,2,\ldots N\}}~.

(123)

At the end of all the extractions, the memory
M_{N}
can then be quasi-statically reset. As previously calculated, as
t\to N
, the distribution of
r_{t}
becomes more peaked and the total entropy of the memory registers scale with
O(\ln N)^{3}
, likewise for the cost of measurement. The resultant dissipation therefore still scales with
O(\polylog(N))
.
